Each row is a locus, not a token count. Changes against a witness count substantive readings, omissions, placement and deletion state; macro syntax and certainty alone do not count as textual changes. Explicit doubt decisions are retained in the page tables. Coordinates refer to the supplied whole-page JPEG, with origin at its upper left. A crop may support adjacent loci in one coherent formula or phrase.

## Consequential corrections

- Scan 013, 013-05: The missing arrow remains a source blank despite the functor terminology.

- Scan 015, 015-01: The proposed definition and its existence condition are cancelled, while on obtient and the following display remain outside the cancellation.

- Scan 015, 015-03: The arrow under Lim is leftward although the nearby text says limite inductive; the edition preserves both.

- Scan 015, 015-23: The cancelled pair has the object e, not a group element g, agreeing with the inserted name of the one-object category.

- Scan 016, 016-05: H supplies a restriction bar that was not inked.

- Scan 016, 016-08: The stability condition remains at its written attachment after the containing clause.

- Scan 016, 016-12: The output V subscript is cancelled in the quotient expression; H leaves it live.

- Scan 016, 016-22: The final category-membership position is uninked; the relation expected from the discussion is not supplied.

- Scan 019, 019-01: The isomorphism sign supplied by H is not inked.

- Scan 019, 019-38: The attachment affects the syntax of the local-endomorphism condition; neither alternative is resolved from expected mathematics.

- Scan 020, 020-27: The first local-object label lacks the i supplied by H.

- Scan 020, 020-28: The second local-object label lacks the lower S prime supplied by H.

### Unresolved readings

- line 4, struck explanation: ` en particulier `; alternatives: `  `; low. Several middle letters remain obscured by x cancellation; the candidate is retained but not silently made certain.

- line 26, raised character after O: ` N `; alternatives: ` n `; low. Narrow uprights and a central dark diagonal or shoulder are indistinct; the elevated position is secure.

- line 26, final struck word: ` typ `; alternatives: ` ty with an unidentified fragment of a third letter `; low. The sheet cuts the next character after ty; p is compatible with its surviving fragment. A final e is not visible and is not supplied.

- line3, overprinted cell in appelerons: ` appel\unsure{\deleted{r}e|\deleted{e}r}rons `; alternatives: ` \deleted{r}e; \deleted{e}r `; medium. There is one tall l. The following cell contains the rounded bowl and middle bar of e together with an extra upright and rightward shoulder matching r, before the separate clear r of rons. Preserve this newly detected r/e composite and both impression orders; do not silently retain plain appelerons.

- line7, struck word after des: ` flêches `; alternatives: ` flèches `; low. The independent flêches/flèches fits the surviving tall l in the second cell, an accent-like dark feature over the third, a high h stem in the fifth, and the following rounded e and s remnants. These features are better than the earlier fibreux/faisceaux/fibrés proposals. The multiple x impressions still obscure the accent and some letter edges, so preserve the two accented candidates as a doubtful deleted word.

### Unresolved readings

- line 6, struck ending after variable: ` \deleted{s} `; alternatives: `  `; medium. The cancelling x is visible; the putative s beneath it is not completely separable.

- line1, typed insertion pleine: ` \unsure{\deleted{n}p|\deleted{p}n}\unsure{\deleted{e}l|\deleted{l}e|\deleted{d}l|\deleted{l}d}eine `; alternatives: ` n/p orders; e/l orders; d/l orders `; low. The first cell has p descender/bowl and an additional n-like right upright with a baseline foot. The next cell combines a tall l with rounded contours and a medial horizontal trace compatible with e; the earlier d remains possible because the main upright masks its right side. Preserve n/p and e/l or d/l composites, with both possible orders. These are two revised cells in one inserted word.

- line6, fourth cell of variable: ` var\unsure{\deleted{s}i|\deleted{i}s}able\deleted{\unsure{s}} `; alternatives: ` s/i orders `; low. The source visibly separates d from essus; retain that literal blank. In variable, the fourth cell has a dotted i upright and a second pair of curved s-like traces. Add the s/i overprint in both orders. The final x still partly obscures the putative deleted s, so retain that separate uncertainty.

- line8, initial struck word after forment: ` un `; alternatives: ` on; ou `; low. The first two cancelled cells retain lower loops and uprights compatible with un, on or ou. Heavy x impressions prevent choosing among them. Extend the earlier doubtful un to include the independent on/ou candidates; les fibres d'une is unchanged.

- line8, end of deleted fibrée: ` s `; alternatives: ` s beneath cancellation; empty cancelled position `; low. The initial live s belongs to the preceding word. The deleted span clearly contains fibrée followed by a heavily crossed position before sur. The final s proposed in the first pass is not individually recoverable, while the second pass treats that position as empty. Mark that s doubtful rather than silently affirming or discarding it. The following sur la caté is clear.

- line21, r(X) notation: ` \unsure{\deleted{r}\underline{r}|\underline{r}|\deleted{f}r|\deleted{r}f}(X) `; alternatives: ` displaced r with separate underline; f/r composite without independently secure underline `; low. There are displaced r-like shoulders, a long upright and two horizontal strokes. The independent f alternative is compatible with a continuous upright plus upper curve/crossbar. The thick low stroke may be a separate underline or a foot belonging to a displaced impression. Keep the prior underlined-r readings and add both r/f orders without a compulsory separate underline in those alternatives.

- line25, ending after se r: ` accorde\unsure{nt|\deleted{m}nt|\deleted{nt}m} `; alternatives: ` nt; deleted m then nt; deleted nt then m `; low. The crowded ending has nt-like strokes and additional short arch/upright material compatible with m. The independent m reading gives a positive candidate for the previously unspecified underlying cell. Retain plain nt and both m/nt overprint orders; do not reconstruct raccorderont.

### Unresolved readings

- base line 3, mark after the large parenthesis: ` \handwritten{\unsure{,|)}} `; alternatives: ` ,; ) `; low. A separate narrow descending black stroke is present, but its exact punctuation value is uncertain.

- base line 4, first underlined slashed-oval symbol; recurs in lines 5 and 8: ` \handwritten{\unsure{\underline{\varnothing}|\underline{\phi}|\underline{\Phi}}} `; alternatives: ` \underline{\varnothing}; \underline{\phi}; \underline{\Phi} `; medium. The diagonal crossing an oval favours varnothing as the graphical form. The hand could use that shape for phi/Phi, and this leaf does not settle the intended alphabet; later occurrences use the first candidate unmarked.

- base line 6, nested struck phrase: ` \unsure{revêtement} `; alternatives: `  `; low. Most letters are readable, but the last position is heavily crossed by typed x marks.

- display, cancelled initial symbol: ` \deleted{\handwritten{\unsure{\underline{s}|\underline{\xi}|\phi|r}}} `; alternatives: ` \underline{s}; \underline{\xi}; \phi; r `; low. The cancelled glyph has a compact sinuous body and a substantial separate-looking low stroke, with a short descending remnant. The expanded original crop(570,850,800,945), saved as scan015_main_audit_deleted_symbol.jpg, admits underlined s or xi as well as the earlier obscured phi/r candidates. Preserve all four locally; no illegible marker is inserted into mathematics. The live r:, low underlined R and blank mapping slot remain unchanged.

- base line21, cancelled first word: ` sur `; alternatives: ` aux `; low. The first cancelled group has clear u and a final shoulder/upright more compatible with r than a standing x. Its first letter is heavily crossed, leaving sur the stronger independent candidate while aux cannot be fully eliminated. Preserve sur/aux alternatives before les objets, whose j descender and remaining letters are clear.

- base line24, verb after nous: ` fo\unsure{\deleted{rm}ur|\deleted{ur}rm}nit `; alternatives: ` deleted rm then ur; deleted ur then rm `; low. The two cells after fo contain low u-like return and multiple r/m shoulders. The independent ur/rm reconstruction supplies specific candidates for the formerly illegible overtyped group. Retain both impression orders as deleted rm then ur, or deleted ur then rm; no interlinear placement is asserted because the strokes occupy the same baseline cells.

- base line25, cancelled fragment before catégorie: ` mét `; alternatives: ` rét; cét `; low. A detached oblique acute is visible above the second cancelled cell; the final tall t and crossbar are clear. The first crossed cell can be m, r or c. Replace the earlier cat proposal, which omits the positive accent, with mét/rét/cét alternatives.

- base line26, third cell of étales: ` ét\unsure{\deleted{q}a|\deleted{a}q}les `; alternatives: ` q/a order; a/q order `; low. The third cell has a q descender below the baseline plus an extra low rightward foot and upper contour compatible with a. Both contours should survive in the transcription. Preserve q/a overprint orders rather than plain étqles or normalized étales.

- base line27, fifth letter of compatible: ` a `; alternatives: ` partly masked a `; low. The position between p and t is dominated by the cancellation x. Faint low curved material permits a but does not independently establish its full bowl and upright. Keep compatible as the candidate with only that a marked doubtful.

### Unresolved readings

- line 7, cancelled word before normaux: ` \deleted{\unsure{de|du}} `; alternatives: ` de; du `; medium. The d is clear, while the second body is crossed by x and can retain either an e trace or a damaged u.

- line 26, low R after quotient comma: ` \mathord{,}_{\unsure{R|\deleted{R}}} `; alternatives: ` live R; \deleted{R} `; medium. R itself is readable. Extra diagonals in its lower body could be cancellation, but a damaged/doubled impression is also possible. Retain both live and struck states for main adjudication.

- line 6, cancelled word after Rev: ` \deleted{\unsure{etd|età|est}} `; alternatives: ` etd; età; est `; low. The expanded crop(1245,535,1335,620) shows an e-like first bowl and a definite tall central t with crossbar. The final cell has a rounded lower bowl and a high hooked stroke, suggesting d, while a plus a grave accent is also possible under the x. Retain etd/età/est as competing cancelled readings, with the positive tall-stem forms first. The live estéquivalente remains joined.

- line 14, de… groupes: ` de\unsure{\deleted{s}|x|s|\deleted{s}x|\deleted{x}s} `; alternatives: ` cancelled s; live x; live s; s/x impression order; x/s impression order `; medium. A rounded middle return is visible beneath the diagonal x in the last cell of des; the expanded crop(1410,1040,1515,1120) confirms it. Add the independent interpretation of a cancelled s, treating x as the cancellation mark. Keep the earlier live x/s and overlapping impression alternatives because the superposition does not wholly establish status or order. The circumflex in systême is visible.

- line 9, first factorization category: ` \underline{\mathrm{F\unsure{\deleted{a}l|\deleted{l}a|\deleted{o}l|\deleted{l}o}op}}_S `; alternatives: ` a/l orders; o/l orders `; medium. - line 25, compatibde/compatible: ` compatib\unsure{\deleted{l}d|\deleted{d}l|\deleted{o}l|\deleted{l}o}e `; alternatives: ` d/l orders; o/l orders `; medium. - line 26, second M in the final flèche formula: ` \unsure{M|\deleted{M}M|\deleted{X}M|\deleted{M}X} `; alternatives: ` repeated M; X/M orders `; low. The target has M-like outer stems and displaced crossing diagonals. They permit a repeated M impression or an X/M overstrike, but do not establish the order or exclude a damaged single M. Add the explicit X/M possibilities locally. The M-prime on the left and C with low S-prime on the right are clear; the map interval is blank.

### Unresolved readings

- regular line 2, opening word: ` Bour `; alternatives: ` Pour; \deleted{P}Bour; \deleted{B}Pour `; medium. The lower rounded component favours B, while dense superposed ink permits a P/B correction; the order is not established.

- regular line 4, complete n-family formula: ` \unsure{\underline{n}=\deleted{-}(n_i)_{i\quad I}|\underline{n}=\deleted{(}-n_i)_{i\quad I}} `; alternatives: ` \underline{n}=\deleted{-}(n_i)_{i\quad I}; \underline{n}=\deleted{(}-n_i)_{i\quad I} `; medium. A parenthesis curve and a separate horizontal key share the opening cell. Both must be represented, with their order unresolved.

- regular line 12, two middle cells of cartésien: ` car\unsure{\deleted{é}t|\deleted{t}é}\unsure{\deleted{t}é|\deleted{é}t}sien `; alternatives: ` car\deleted{é}t\deleted{t}ésien; car\deleted{t}é\deleted{é}tsien `; medium. Both cells contain t and é components. The two in-place marks leave each layer order independently open.

- regular line 16, first letter of flêches: ` \deleted{c}flêches `; alternatives: ` \deleted{f}clêches `; medium. A curved c outline and tall f/crossbar coincide. The circumflex is clear, but the order of the initial letters is not.

- regular line 22, full cancelled covering-map formula: ` S\quad S `; alternatives: ` S'\quad S `; medium. The first S is clear, but there is no independently secure prime among the x cancellations. The prime possibility is retained.

- regular line 22, first cell of sur/éur: ` \deleted{é}sur `; alternatives: ` \deleted{s}éur; śur `; medium. The cell retains an acute mark, an e-like bar and an s-like lower bowl. The exact layers and their order cannot be fixed.

- regular line 9, word after vide: ` s\unsure{\deleted{i}a|\deleted{a}i}uf `; alternatives: ` i/a orders `; medium. The mark above a is round and aligned with a narrow vertical impression running through the a body. Together these support a superposed i rather than a detached acute or grave alone. Record both i/a impression orders; the a outline, u and f are clear.

- regular line 10, second m of morphismes: ` morphis\unsure{m|n}es,et `; alternatives: ` n `; medium. The weak central shoulder of the second m is less distinct than the surrounding strokes and permits n. Keep m first but mark m/n locally. The comma directly abuts et; the final l belongs to the following la and is already in the page.

- regular line 15, cancelled cell within façon: ` faç\deleted{\unsure{a|o|x}}on `; alternatives: ` o; x `; medium. Inside the crossed cell after ç, the top hooked portion and lower rounded body also permit a, rather than only the former o/x readings. Add a as the leading cancelled candidate while retaining o and x because the cancelling diagonals obscure the central bar. The live on is separate.

- regular line 19, initial of normal: ` \unsure{\deleted{j}n|\deleted{i}n|\deleted{n}j|\deleted{n}i}ormal `; alternatives: ` j/n orders; i/n orders `; medium. The n arch, high dot and low extra stroke remain superposed. A j/n combination is strongest, but an i/n impression is not wholly excluded. The same uncertainty about order applies to that i/n pair, so add the reversed n/i order alongside the existing three alternatives.

- inserted qualification after line 20, fifth letter of composante: ` comp\unsure{o|c}sante `; alternatives: ` c `; medium. The fifth letter of composante has an open right side; weak type could make it o, but c is also admissible, so mark o/c. In de’un, two crossing diagonal strokes extend through the e beyond its curved body and central bar. They establish its cancellation; resolve the earlier live/deleted e alternatives as deleted e.

- regular line 24, cancelled underlined capital: ` \deleted{$\unsure{\underline{Q}|\underline{\Omega}|\underline{\delta}|\underline{D}|\underline{O}}$} `; alternatives: ` \Omega; \delta; D; O `; low. The cancelled underlined symbol has a rounded body and a high curved remnant, with its lower outline obscured by x strokes. These admit the independently proposed delta/D/O forms as well as the former Q/Omega candidates; no unique alphabet can be established. Add those alternatives. The separate live symbol on the right has an oval extending below its horizontal crossbar with a returning lower-right arc, supporting theta rather than e. The raised (D_i) stays above the cancelled left symbol.

- regular line 24, complete divisor equation: ` \unsure{D_{S\overset{=}{'}}=\quad D_i|D_{S\deleted{=}'}=\quad D_i|D_{S\deleted{'}=} =\quad D_i|D_{S\bar{'}}=\quad D_i} `; alternatives: ` live extra equals; cancelled equals/prime order; overbarred prime `; medium. The low prime has a broad head, with an additional horizontal stroke above it, and a separate equals farther right. The lower of the apparent extra pair merges with the prime head; an overbarred prime is therefore also possible. Add that representation to the three prior live/cancelled equals alternatives. Preserve the whole formula and its blank position before D_i.

### Unresolved readings

- lines 26–27, qu' serait: ` qu' serait `; alternatives: `  `; high. Source slip, not an uncertain reading: no il follows the apostrophe at the line end or precedes serait on the next line. The missing letters are not supplied.

- line 4, cancelled passage after schéma: ` \deleted{co\unsure{n|u}v\unsure{r}} `; alternatives: ` u for n; last r remains uncertain `; low. The first c has an open curved body and the second cell has an oval o rim underneath the x. The next cell has uprights and an uncertain n/u joining structure; the fourth has a v-shaped valley. The last cell has an r-like shoulder and foot, still too crossed for certainty. Read co[n/u]v[r?], keeping those local doubts instead of treating cancellation x characters as the underlying word.

- line 8, cancelled cell after au: ` au\deleted{\unsure{g|q}} `; alternatives: ` q `; low. The cancelled character has a dark curved remnant descending below the common baseline, with a lower leftward return. This favours g, with q still possible; plain x or s does not account for that descender. Preserve g/q without completing the word from grammar.

- line 9, composite in plusieurs: ` p\unsure{\deleted{h}l|\deleted{l}h|\deleted{d}l|\deleted{l}d}usieurs `; alternatives: ` h/l and d/l overprint orders `; medium. The shared cell has the tall l head and stem, an h-like right shoulder, and a curved remnant to the left of the stem that also permits d. Add the two d/l overprint orders while retaining h/l in both orders; the sequence of the impressions cannot be recovered.

- line 22, parenthesized reference key: ` \unsure{(*)|(\pi)|(\deleted{=}x)|(\deleted{x}=)} `; alternatives: ` pi; x/equals overprint orders `; low. The key contains four small x-like feet/diagonals together with a dense horizontal middle band. A clean K lacks the required independent left stem, and a clean X does not explain the band. Retain the unresolved star/pi readings and add x/equals overprint alternatives in both orders; the crop does not establish their chronology.

### Unresolved readings

- line 2, full product definition and its vertically displaced S bodies: ` Z'=Zx_{\unsure{\substack{S\\S}|\substack{\deleted{S}\\S}|\substack{S\\\deleted{S}}}}S' `; alternatives: ` \substack{\deleted{S}\\S}; \substack{S\\\deleted{S}} `; medium. Two S bodies at different low levels are secure. Their order of typing and whether either was intended to replace the other are not established by an independent strike. Both impressions remain visible in the transcription; no blackboard S is asserted.

- line 4, full u formula with cancelled upper key: ` u:Z'_o/H_1^{\deleted{\unsure{1|I}}}\quad Z'_2/H_2 `; alternatives: ` I `; low. The upper key beside H is genuinely x-cancelled. Its remaining vertical stroke does not decide 1 versus capital I. The lower live 1, blank map position and target component 2 are independently visible and retained.

- line 14, full underlined conditions word: ` \uline{condition\unsure{\deleted{\unsure{g|p}}s|\deleted{s}\unsure{g|p}|s|g|p}} `; alternatives: ` \deleted{s}\unsure{g|p}; s; g; p `; low. The s-shaped upper body and the extra lower descender/foot may represent overtyped s with g or p. The underline intersects the lower body, so neither the second letter nor the chronology is secure. Both layer orders and the unresolved single-letter alternatives are retained.

- line 16, terminal part of cancelled wording at the cropped edge: ` \deleted{for morphis\unsure{m}} `; alternatives: `  `; medium. The cancellation’s x at the word space is not treated as an old literal x. Most of for morphism is recoverable, but the final m is clipped at the right edge and remains tentative. Nothing beyond that visible remnant is completed.

- line 21, composite capital/lower-case initial after whether: ` \unsure{\deleted{G}g|\deleted{g}G}alois `; alternatives: ` \deleted{g}G `; medium. Both a capital G body and a lower-case g with its lower loop are visible. Their overtyping order cannot be recovered from the overlapping ink.

- line 4, first composite cell in the morphism word: ` i\unsure{\deleted{s}d|\deleted{d}s}\unsure{\deleted{i}o|\deleted{o}i|\deleted{h}o|\deleted{o}h}morphisme `; alternatives: ` d/s orders; next cell i/o or h/o `; medium. The additional upper terminal, stem and crossing material support the earlier i reading, but the blind f/r alternatives cannot be excluded from the superposed strokes. Preserve all locally plausible identities and both layer orders; no clean by is substituted.

- line8, isolated character after method: ` \unsure{I|t} `; alternatives: ` I; t `; medium. The isolated character has a broad lower serif and I-like upright, but its damaged head carries a short upright above a low bar that also permits t. Retain I first with t as an explicit alternative; the ordinary t in method has a different lower return, so t is not promoted to certainty.
